```json
    "type": "skill_listing",
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",
```

```text
{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_17/projects/recovery_aware_struct_prune/memory.md\", \"content\": \"# Memory\\n\\n## 2026-05-16: α Ablation Sweep westb (cal=512) — 非单调 U 形确认\\n- α=0.0→27%, α=0.25→23%, α=0.5→22%, α=1.0→31%, α=2.0→36%\\n- 与 westd (cal=256) 对比：两者都呈非单调 U 形。小 α (0.25-0.5) 引入噪声反而更差\\n- α=1.0 是精度-生成质量最佳平衡（31%, EOS 92%, avg_len 733）\\n- α=2.0 精度最高但 EOS 仅 59%——可能是\\\"暴力搜索\\\"（长输出碰运气）\\n- **Claim 2 支撑：α 显著影响 post-prune reasoning retention，最优区间 α∈[1.0, 2.0]**\\n\\n## 2026-05-16: sft_unpruned_baseline — 全量模型 SFT 基线\\n- Unpruned SFT: loss 0.332, token_acc 0.909（vs 剪枝模型 loss 0.64, token_acc 0.83）\\n- 全量参数模型学习能力明显更强，8pp token_accuracy 差异\\n- N=100 eval 运行中，结果待收集\\n\\n## 2026-05-16: RASP GRPO v9 — save_steps=5 捕获 collapse 前 peak\\n- PID 566666, westb cuda:0, wandb mrikafz3\\n- save_steps=5, save_total_limit=5, max_steps=30\\n- 预计 step 5/10 有 checkpoint（RASP collapse onset ~step 10）\\n- 这是论文 RASP vs Standard GRPO 对比的关键缺失数据\\n\\n## 2026-05-15: N=1319 Full Eval — RASP vs Standard SFT 无显著差异\\n- westd N=1319: Standard 59.29% ±1.35%, RASP 61.49% ±1.34%\\n- Δ(RASP - Standard) = +2.20pp, z=1.154, **p=0.248, 不显著**, 95% CI = [-1.53, +5.93]pp\\n- **结论：post-SFT 两种 pruning 方法恢复能力无统计差异，论文 Claim 不能基于 SFT accuracy 差异**\\n\\n## 2026-05-15: Unpruned GRPO baseline COLLAPSE — 改变 narrative\\n- Unpruned DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B 在相同 GRPO v8 参数下也 collapse：peak reward 0.044 (step 20) → step 80 collapse → step 100 全零\\n- **结论：GRPO collapse 不是剪枝导致的，是当前 GRPO 配置本身的问题**\\n- Collapse 时间线：RASP (step ~10) < Standard (step ~60) < Unpruned (step ~80)\\n- **Standard pruning 是唯一延迟 collapse 最久的** — counterintuitive\\n\\n## 2026-05-15: 文献搜索 — Pruning + RL Stability (Narrative A 支撑)\\n- **无直接先例**: 没有论文报告 pruning → GRPO/RLVR collapse。强新颖性。\\n- **Top 引用**: ①Dohare Nature 2024 ②Ma ICML 2025 ③Obando-Ceron ICML 2024 ④Zheng GSPO 2025\\n\\n## 2026-05-15: RASP GRPO 3x collapse + 零 checkpoint\\n- RASP GRPO v2/v5/v8 全部 collapse，v2-v8 全版本零 checkpoint（save_steps 大于 collapse 步数）\\n- v9 (save_steps=5) 是修复方案\\n\\n## 2026-05-15: 三方 post-SFT 对比 — 论文核心数据点\\n- **Standard 64% > RASP 57% > GraSP 41%** (N=100, greedy, seed=42, westb)\\n- GraSP vs Standard: p=0.001，统计显著\\n- α ablation: SFT 下 α 无显著趋势（α=0.0 64%, α=1.0 57%, α=2.0 62%, α=4.0 62%）\\n\\n## 2026-05-15: 代码与环境备忘\\n- westd eval 脚本必须从 westb 同步（含 apply_chat_template），旧版在 /root/autodl-tmp/ 下\\n- sft_recovery.py `--gpu` 参数覆盖外层 CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES（第 19-22 行）——启动时直接传正确 --gpu N\\n- eval_gsm8k.py 默认: seed=42, greedy\\n- westb 磁盘：清理后可用空间充足（旧 RASP GRPO v2-v8 目录已清理）\\n\", \"total_chars\": 2298, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}
```

```text
- Status: ANALYZING, conclusion: success  


- Metrics: accuracy=81.27%, correct=1072/1319, eos_rate=99.7%, avg_response_length=209, seed=42
- Server: westd-11672, GPU: cuda:0

Both evaluations show the unpruned GRPO v2 model performing surprisingly well at steps 90 and 100 with accuracies around 80-81%, which contradicts what I noted in memory.md about the model collapsing and outputting all zeros at step 100. This discrepancy is worth investigating further.

Let me clarify the experiment setup — this is `eval_grpo_unpruned_v2_step90` from the `grpo_unpruned_v2` run that completed successfully on 2026-05-16. The key insight here is that despite the reward collapse documented in the collapse timeline, the model maintains strong accuracy performance, which aligns with the reward-accuracy decoupling finding mentioned in the roadmap.

Comparing across the different approaches, the unpruned GRPO v2 achieves 80%+ accuracy at steps 90 and 100, substantially outperforming the standard v8 baseline at 58.91% and the RASP v9 variant at 68%. This decoupling between reward signals and task accuracy appears to be the core phenomenon I'm investigating.

Both unpruned experiments have now completed successfully with strong accuracy metrics, so I need to mark them as done and update the documentation. I should also synthesize what this means for the broader project narrative around GRPO's effectiveness. I need to pull the RASP v9 step 5 results to complete the comparison, then mark both the unpruned v2 step 90 and step 100 experiments as done before diving into the resource-saturation analysis.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have the full picture. Let me compile the key data:

**Unpruned GRPO v2 training**: 
- Peak reward 0.669 at step 80, final reward 0.661 at step 100
- NOT collapsed — reward stayed high! Checkpoints: 90, 95, 100
- This is DIFFERENT from the original unpruned baseline (which collapsed)

**Unpruned GRPO v2 evals** (both in ANALYZING, need to move to DONE):
- Step 90: 80.59% (1063/1319)


- Step 100: 81.27% (1072/1319)

**Unpruned SFT baseline**: 81.27% (eval_sft_unpruned_full)

So unpruned GRPO v2 at step 100 matches the SFT baseline exactly at 81.27%, while step 90 is slightly lower at 80.59%. This means GRPO isn't actually improving accuracy on the unpruned model — it's essentially flat compared to SFT. The pruned models, though, show a completely different story with much more dramatic shifts between SFT and GRPO performance.

Looking at the reward-accuracy relationship, I'm noticing something important: the unpruned GRPO v2 peaked in reward at step 80 (0.669) but maintained accuracy around 80.59-81.27% without collapsing, which contradicts what I initially remembered about the first unpruned baseline. That earlier run was invalid anyway due to missing the SFT step, so this v2 version is actually the reliable one.

The pattern is becoming clearer now—pruning dramatically increases susceptibility to GRPO collapse, with RASP pruning being particularly unstable, while the unpruned model stays robust throughout training.

I'm noticing the reward-accuracy decoupling across both conditions: the unpruned model gains reward without sacrificing accuracy, but the pruned models show collapse in reward while accuracy holds up better than expected. Now I need to finalize these experiment records and update the checkpoint and log paths for the director.

Looking at the GPU allocation, I see that the westd GPUs just freed up from the evaluation jobs, so there's capacity available for the next phase of work.
```

```text
- Unpruned GRPO v2 step 90: 80.59%

- → **GRPO on unpruned model does NOT improve accuracy over SFT** — accuracy flat at ~81%
- → But reward went from ~0.4 to 0.669 — reward-accuracy decoupling


- Pruned models tell a different story: both the standard and RASP variants collapse under GRPO, dropping from their baseline SFT accuracies within the first few steps, while the unpruned model remains stable throughout training. This suggests the unpruned model is learning to exploit the reward signal without actually improving task performance, whereas pruned models can't sustain this gaming behavior and degrade instead.

Now I'm checking the current GPU allocation across the workers to see what resources are available for the next round of experiments.

The key insight I need to communicate to the Director is that GRPO achieves reward optimization without any corresponding improvement in reasoning accuracy — the model is essentially learning to satisfy the reward signal through format gaming rather than genuine reasoning improvements. I should update my notes with this finding and then decide how to allocate the free GPU capacity. I'm seeing 3 GPUs sitting idle while 5 workers are running, though I can't confirm whether they're actively using those free resources or just queued up waiting for them. The workers appear to be SFT training and evaluation tasks that would need GPU access, so there's potential for better resource utilization here.

Now I need to finalize the two experiment updates, prepare the report with the eval results and comparisons to the SFT baseline, update my notes, and figure out what to do with those free GPUs. I'm also noticing something interesting in the RASP v9 step 10 early metrics — if the final accuracy lands around 65-70%, that would actually be higher than the pre-GRPO SFT baseline of 61.49%, which would suggest GRPO improved accuracy despite the collapse issue.

Let me check the decision log to see what's already been documented. D045 and D044 are already recorded, so the Director knows about the reward-accuracy decoupling and the narrative pivot. The new step 90 and step 100 results (80.59% and 81.27% respectively) are the complete confirmation of that decoupling pattern. I should just report these efficiently and move forward rather than overthinking the framing.

For the resource status, I'm seeing one eval job running on westb:0, five workers active on CPU tasks that are still in their setup phase after being spawned a couple minutes ago, and some free GPU slots.
```
